# Financial Risk Management: question papers in full

57 unique papers · 2010-11 to 2025-26 · Common (all branches), Computer Engineering, Mechanical, Information Technology, Data Science, Chemical, Electronics and Telecommunication, Artificial Intelligence

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

````text
Q-1   b. An Indian automobile company provides a 90-day credit period to its international   [03]

BL:   increased.
Remember   Identify the financial risk involved and discuss its implications on the company’s

Q-1   c. Shree Silver Works, a silver jewellery manufacturer, requires 50 kilograms of silver   [03]
CO:2   on September 1. On June 1, the spot price of silver is ₹90 per gram.
BL: Apply   The firm plans to enter a forward contract to buy silver with delivery on September
1. The total storage cost for holding the silver is estimated to be ₹1,20,000, and this
storage cost must be paid immediately on June 1.
The company can earn 9% per annum as the risk-free rate on its funds elsewhere.
Calculate the theoretical forward price of silver per gram on June 1 for delivery on
September 1 using the Cost of Carry Model.
Q-2   a. The current value of the NIFTY 50 is 23,400. The following call options on NIFTY   [04]
50 are trading in the market:
CO:2   Strike Price (₹)   23,000 23,300 23,500 23,800
BL:
Understand   Call Premium (₹)   520   330   210   120
Requirement:
Calculate the Intrinsic Value and Time Value of each call option.
Q-2   b. Company PQR Ltd. and LMN Ltd. face the following borrowing rates in the   [06]
market:
CO:2   Borrowing Type   PQR Ltd.   LMN Ltd.
BL:   USD (Floating Rate) LIBOR + 0.4% LIBOR + 2.2%
Apply   Euro (Fixed Rate)   3.0%   3.8%
Assume that LMN Ltd. wishes to borrow USD at a floating rate, while PQR Ltd.
wants to borrow Euro at a fixed rate. A financial intermediary arranges an interest
rate swap between the two firms and charges 0.50% (50 basis points) as its fee for
facilitating the swap. The remaining benefit from the swap, after deducting the
intermediary’s fee, is equally shared between PQR Ltd. and LMN Ltd.
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Calculate the effective interest rate each firm will pay after the swap when the
remaining benefit is equally shared between the two firms.
Q-3   a. A mutual fund portfolio has a market value of ₹250 million invested in a diversified   [05]
set of equities with a beta of 0.75 relative to the Nifty 50 index. The Nifty 50 index
CO: 3
BL:   is currently at 17,800 points. A three-month Nifty 50 futures contract is trading at
Analyze
17,920 points, and the beta of the futures contract is 1. The rupee multiplier for the
contract is 75.
a) How many Nifty 50 futures contracts should the portfolio manager trade to
increase the portfolio beta from 0.75 to 1.30?
b) Suppose market conditions change and the manager now wants to fully hedge the
portfolio and reduce the beta from 0.75 to zero. How many futures contracts would
be required?
Also indicate whether the manager should buy or sell the futures contracts in each
case. Briefly interpret how these strategies affect the portfolio’s exposure to market
risk.
Q-3   b. FreshBrew Coffee Ltd., a beverage company, expects to purchase 80,000 kg of   [05]
coffee beans in four months. However, coffee futures are not available in the
CO: 3   exchange it uses. Therefore, the company decides to cross hedge using cocoa
BL:   futures, since cocoa prices are closely related to coffee prices.
Apply   The following information is available:
•   Current spot price of coffee beans = ₹320 per kg
•   Correlation between changes in coffee prices and cocoa futures prices =
0.78
•   Standard deviation of coffee price changes = ₹18 per kg
•   Standard deviation of cocoa futures price changes = ₹24 per kg
•   Size of one cocoa futures contract = 5,000 kg
•   Current cocoa futures price = ₹290 per kg
Required
Calculate the optimal hedge ratio, determine the position (long or short) FreshBrew
Coffee Ltd. should take in cocoa futures, and compute the number of futures
contracts required for hedging.
Q-4   a. Explain what theta measures for an option. Why is theta important for option sellers? [04]

• Time to maturity = 6 months
•   Risk-free interest rate = 8% per annum
********** 2 **********

•   Call option premium = ₹25
(a) Using the put-call parity relationship, calculate the theoretical price of the
corresponding European put option.
(b) If the actual market price of the put option is ₹18, examine whether an arbitrage
opportunity exists. Briefly explain the arbitrage strategy, without computation.
Q-5   a. The current market price of Mahindra & Mahindra Ltd. stock is ₹2900. A European   [06]
call option on this stock has an exercise price of ₹2850 and an expiry of 3 months.
CO: 2   In 3 months, the stock price can either increase by 7% or decrease by 5%.
BL: Apply   The risk-free interest rate is 6.5% per annum.
Using the one-period (3-month) binomial option pricing model, calculate the
current price of the call option.
Q-5   b. An investor takes a long position in one Nifty 50 futures contract at 18,200 points.   [04]
The contract multiplier is 75. The initial margin is ₹1,00,000 and the maintenance
CO: 1,3   margin is ₹75,000.
BL:   At the end of each trading day, the settlement prices are as follows:
Understand   • Day 1: 18,300
• Day 2: 17,700
(a) Calculate the daily mark-to-market (MTM) gain or loss and margin account
balance for Day 1 and Day 2.
(b) Determine the margin call on Day 2 and the amount the investor must deposit to
restore the margin to the initial level.
Q-6   a. A trader is considering buying a call option on Tata Steel Ltd. currently trading at   [05]
₹1,200. The risk-free rate is 6% per annum, and the stock has a volatility of 25% per

BL:   annum. Assume the stock will not pay any dividend over the next 6 months. The
Apply
strike price of the option is ₹1,250. Using the Black–Scholes model, determine the
fair price of the 6-month European call option for the trader.
Q-6   b. Explain the role of derivatives in risk management. How can instruments such as   [05]
````

````text
QI. DHFL shares are quoting at INR 85, while a 3-month future contrdct is being traded at INR 86. Prevailing interest
rate is 12%. Is there any arbitrage opportunity available considering that DHFL is not going to pay any dividend in next
3 months? How can one make profit considering the lot size as 100 shares? What will be the total profit? (12 Marks)

Q2. A stock is trading at Rs 105.00. You are willing to write a call on the stock exercisable at the end of 3 months with
strike price of Rs 110.00. If the risk free rate of interest is 12% p.a. and stock has exhibited volatility of 30% based on
the past data, what premium would you like to charge for writing the call and Put? (12 Marks)

Q3. What is box spread? Explain with a suitable example. (12 Marks)

Q4. What do you understand by Delta of an option? How it is measured for call and put options? Describe its behavior
with respect to spot price. (12 Marks)

Q5. What are the determinants of Options prices? How would option price change with changes in these
determinants? (12 Marks)

Q6. a) What do you understand by long hedge and short hedge? Illustrate. (6+6=12 Marks)
Q6. b) A refinery uses crude oil as a major input. The current price of crude oil is INR 4000 per barrel. 3-month
Future contract is traded at INR 4150. Lot size is 100 barrel and cost of capital is 12%. And annual storage cost is 3%.
Ill. Do you think the crude oil futures is fairly prices?
IV. If there is an arbitrage opportunity, how can it be executed.

a) Assumptions of BSM
b) Exotic Options
````

````text
1. Please select whether the following statement is true or false. (1*12=12)

I. Difference of spot price and futures price would shrink as futures reaches
maturity.

TI. Futures price would always be higher than spot price because net cost of
carry can never be negative.

II. Higher the volatility of the price of underlying asset higher would be the
initial margin in a futures contract.
IV. Initial margin is not required in the forward contract because forward price
in not allowed to change.
V. Hedging with futures is not useful because price risk is replaced by basis risk.
VI. The strategy of reverse cash and carry arbitrage is not possible for commodities.
VII. | Commodity futures can never be settled by delivery because of too much variety
available in the commodities.
VIII. Index futures can be settled by delivery of stocks worth the value of futures.
TX. Index futures enable speculation on the stock market.
X. Index futures are an ideal tool for hedging a well diversified portfolio.
XI. Investing in index stocks provides market return and keeps research cost low.
XII. Index futures can eliminate systematic and unsystematic risk.
````

````text
Ql. a Name the different factors that drive the option price and explain clearly how they will
affect the price of call and put options.
b. On January 1, you buy 16,000 shares of Jet Airways at INR 1,000, and you are concerned about a
decrease in the price of Jet Airways shares. There is a March futures contract available. The risk-free
rate is 6%, and Jet Airways is expected to pay a dividend of INR 90 on January 31. The contract in
March matures on March 28. Calculate the futures price on January 1. On March 28, Jet Airways
shares are selling at INR 976. If you hedge your portfolio with Jet Airways futures and sell your
shares on March 28, what will be the realized value of your hedged portfolio?

= (6+6)

Q2.a. State the assumptions of Black Scholes Model and explain their implication.

b. Assume that a security is selling at INR 400 and call and put options are available on the stock with
a maturity of 90 days and an exercise price of INR 420. The call is selling at INR 6, and the risk-free
rate is 8% per annum. According to put-call parity, what should the put sell for? Assume that the
stock does not pay any dividends during the life of the option. (6+6)

88 on March 1. Call options and put options are available with expiry on April 29 and an exercise
price of INR 90. It is expected that the Allahabad Bank share price will be either INR 95 or INR 80.
The risk-free rate is 8%. By using the binomial options pricing model, calculate the call option price

pa on March 1.

25%. The risk free rate is 7% per annum. Find out the price of call option bought by you
using BS model.Also find the price of put option by applying put call parity relationship.
````

````text
Ql. (1*12=12)

1. Following sets of underlying assets can be classified as commodity futures
a) Gold, Tea, NIFTY, and US dollar

b) Gold, Tea, Lead, and Wheat

c) Gold, Tea, British pound, and US dollar

d) Gold, Silver, Tea, and US dollar

2. In case of commodity futures cash and carry arbitrage and reverse cash and carry
arbitrage

a) Only lower bound on futures price

b) Only upper bound on the futures price

c) Both lower and upper bounds on futures price
d) Neither lower nor upper bound on futures price

3. A short hedge means taking a

a) Long position in futures with long position of asset
b) Short position in futures with long position of asset
c) Long position in futures with short position of asset
d) Short position in futures with short position of asset:

4/5
--- page 2 ---
4. Along hedge means taking a

a) Long position in futures with long position of asset
b) Short position in futures with long position of asset
c) Long position in futures with short position of asset
d) Short position in futures with short position of asset

5.A perfect hedge is one where

a) Futures position always results in profit

b) Physical position and futures position are on an identical asset

c) Loss/profit of futures exactly offsets profit/loss of underlying position

d) Prices move exactly as expected

6. Under futures hedge, the price that is locked-in is close to the
a) Price of the closing futures contract

b) Average price of opening and closing futures contracts

c) Higher of the price of opening and closing futures contracts
d) Price of the opening futures contract

7. Index futures are cash settled because

a) Index cannot be delivered

b) Index cannot be constructed

c) Index is only a measure and not a financial asset

d) Index specifications vary from exchange to exchange

8. The cost of carry for pricing stock/index futures comprise of
a) Financing cost multiplied by dividend yield

b) Financing cost divided by dividend yield

c) Financing cost plus dividend yield

d) Financing cost less dividend yield

9. If spot price of Nifty index is 6000, financing cost is 1% per month and dividend yield on
index is 6% per annum, then fair value of 3-m futures on Nifty is

a) 6420
b) 6090
c) 6060
d) 6105

2/5
--- page 3 ---
10. Hedging a diversified portfolio acquired at Rs 90 lacs with current market value worth
Rs 100 lacs with beta of 1.3 can be fully hedged with position in index futures worth

a) Rs 100 lacs

b) Rs 130 lacs

c) Rs 90 lacs

dy Rs 117 lacs

11. Hedging position in stock of Reliance Industries with index future would
a) Eliminate all risk

b) Eliminate systematic risk but have unsystematic risk

c) Eliminate unsystematic risk but have systematic risk

d) Eliminate none of the risk

12. Optimal hedge ratio for hedging a portfolio/stock is equal to
a) One

b) Beta of the portfolio

c) Value of the portfolio

d) Value of portfolio divided by value of index

Q2. Differentiate between:
(a) Interest rate swaps and Credit default swaps
(b) Options and Futures

(6+6)
Q3. You have written a call on exchange rate of US dollar with Rupee with the strike price of Rs
50/$ charging a premium of Rs 1.00. Find the payoff at various exchange rates ranging from Rs
45 to 55. At what levels of exchange rate you would turn from profit to loss? (12)
````

````text
SVKM’s NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCHOOL OF TECHNOLOGY MANAGEMENT & ENGINEERING

Programme: MBA Tech (ALL STREAMS) Year: V Semester: X
Academic Year: 2014-15

Batch: 2014-15

Subject : Financial Risk Management

Date : 01/07/2015
Re-Examination

Instruction: Candidates should read carefully the instructions printed on the question paper and on the
cover of the Answer Book, which is provided for their use.

NB:

1) Question No. 1 is compulsory.

2) Out of remaining questions, attempt any 4 questions.

3) In all 5 questions to be attempted.

4) All questions carry equal marks. ,

5) Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
6) Figures in brackets on the right hand side indicate full marks.
7) Assume Suitable data if necessary

Ql. (1*12=12)
1.Risk is a function of
a) Probability of adverse event happening
b) Amount of loss if the adverse event happens
c) Both a) and b)
d) = Only a)

2.Derivatives and manage risk of

a) Large losses with frequent occurrence
b) — Small losses with frequent occurrence
c) Large losses with infrequent occurrence
d) Small losses with infrequent occurrence

3. Derivatives manage risk for risk-averse people by
a) Diffusing losses to many

b) Transferring it to risk takers

c) Eliminating it from the environment

d) Insuring against the losses
--- page 2 ---
4. Derivative is defined as

a) A financial product that is traded in the market

b) A financial product that guarantees profit

c) A financial product that derives its value from some other underlying asset
d) A financial product that guarantees returns more than the underlying asset

5. Following derivatives are exchange traded
a) Forwards and futures

b) Futures and Swaps

c) Futures and Options

d) Options and Swaps

6. Following derivatives are traded over-the-counter
a) Forwards and futures
b) Futures and Swaps
c) Futures and Options
d) Forwards and Swaps

7. A forward contract is best defined as

a) Apromise to deliver an asset in future at price fixed then
b) A promise to deliver an asset in future at price fixed now
c) A promise to buyback an asset in future at price fixed then
d) Apromise to buyback an asset in future at price fixed now

8. Which of the following sets of features does not belong to the forward contract?
a) OTC product, counterparty risk, and no upfront payment

b) OTC product, no counterparty risk, and no upfront payment

c) OTC product, counterparty risk, and upfront payment

d) Exchange traded product, counterparty risk, and no upfront payment

9. Under a forward contract, there is a commitment to deliver the asset
a) At more than a predetermined price

b) Ata predetermined price

c) At less than a predetermined price

d) Ata price prevailing at the time of delivery

10. Under a forward contract

a) No party gains or loses

b) Both parties gain equally

c) One party gains and the other loses equally
d) Both parties share the gain in desired ratio

2/3
--- page 3 ---
11. A forward contract is used for freezing the

a) Price, quality, quantity and time of delivery, of the underlying asset
b) Price, quality, and quantity of the underlying asset

c) Price, and quality of the underlying asset

d) Price of the underlying asset

12. Both forward and futures contracts are

a) Adjustable price contracts for adjustable date of settlement
b) Adjustable price contracts for fixed date of settlement

c) Fixed price contracts for adjustable date of settlement

d) Fixed price contracts for a fixed date of settlement

Q.2 Write short notes on (12)
(a) Option Greeks ‘
(b) Put-Call Parity
(c) Different risks on derivative instruments

Q.3 Discuss different strategies of option trading? (12)

Q.4 Explain the concept of Delta Hedging with example? (12)

Q5. Suppose a 6-m forward contract on shares of ITC Limited is available. The current market
price of ITC is Rs 180 and risk free interest is s 6% per annum.
A) What should be the price of the 6 month forward contract?
B) What would be the value of the forward contract if a) the interest rate were continuously
compounded
C) what would be the value of the forward contract if a) the interest rate were continuously
compounded and ITC declared dividend of Rs 2.00 payable after 2 months. (4+4+4)

Q6. The spot value of NIFTY is 4,800. With risk free interest rate at 8% and dividend yield on
the 50 shares consisting NIFTY at 4%.
A) What should be the fair value of futures on NIFTY with a) 1 month b) 2 months and c) 3
months to maturity?
B) NIFTY futures with 1-month, 2-months and 3-months maturity are trading at 4,820,
4,825 and 4,855 respectively. What strategies can you adopt with each of the futures contract
to profit? (6+6)

3/3
````

````text
1. The CMP of one stock is Rs 80 and it is expected that stock price after one year will be Rs 72 or Rs 88.
Calculate the value of the Put option of the stock if the exercise price is Rs. 88. Find the probability of
stock price increase and decrease. Assume Risk free rate is 10 %. (Use Binomial Methods) (10)

2. A stock currently trades at Rs 360, its volatility is 25%. Calculate the price of the 3 month European
put option as per Black — Scoles Model if strike price is Rs 392 and risk free rate of interest is 8%. (10)

3. The settlement price of Nifty futures contract on a particular day was Rs. 6150. The initial margin is set
at Rs. 15000, while the maintenance margin is fixed at Rs. 10000. The multiple of each contract is 50.
The settlement price of following five days is 6250, 6300, 6350, 6250 & 6400. Calculate the mark-to-
market cash flows and daily closing prices for long and short position. (10)

4. a) Ms. Zena, an option trader writes a single naked put option. The option premium is Rs 12. The stock
price and exercise price are 269 and 279 respectively. Calculate margin requirement of Ms. Zena. (5)

b) Explain in brief about time value and intrinsic value of option premium. , (5)
5. Explain the various factors influencing option price. (10)
6. What are the merits and demerits of Futures contract? (10)
7. Write a short note on any two (5*2=10 Marks)

(a) Marking to market
(b) Unsystematic risk
(C) Interest rate risk
````

````text
A. A trader owns 1,00,000-units of a particular asset and decides to
hedge the value of her position with futures contracts on another
related asset. Each futures contract is on 10,000 units. The spot price
of the asset that is owned is Rs.75 and the standard deviation of the
change in this price over the life of the hedge is estimated to be 8.5%.
The futures price of the related asset is Rs. 84 and the standard
deviation of the change in this over the life of the hedge is 9.5%. The
coefficient of correlation between the spot price change and futures
price change is 0.95. What is the minimum variance hedge ratio?
Should the hedger take a long or short futures position? Determine
the number of contracts to be considered in futures for hedging.

option with an exercise price of Rs. 75 is selling for Rs. 4.50 and a
3-month put of Rs.75 is selling for Rs. 3.80. The risk-free rate is
5%. Calculate the no-arbitrage price of the put option, and illustrate
how the violation of put-call parity can be exploited to earn
arbitrage profits.
````
